<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md"></a>

<!-- Source: 20-engineering/20-data/030-plan-target-resolution.md -->

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--plan-target-resolution"></a>
# Plan target resolution

Maintenance Plan targets are authored through explicit canonical selections or typed governed selectors, previewed as non-authoritative candidate coverage, resolved atomically during protected publication, and preserved as immutable published targets and trigger bindings.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--keep-target-kind-and-trigger-interpretation-unambiguous"></a>
## Keep target kind and trigger interpretation unambiguous

One Maintenance Plan Revision has exactly one target kind:

- Functional Location;
- Asset.

The target kind is part of revision meaning and cannot vary row by row inside one published revision.

A Plan requiring both kinds is represented by separate Plans unless Product Authority introduces another explicit typed contract.

Measurement Point-triggered Plans additionally preserve one compatible trigger Measurement Point for every resolved target. Trigger identity is therefore part of published target resolution rather than a later lookup against mutable Plant data.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--explicit-target-selection"></a>
## Explicit target selection

Explicit selection accepts canonical target identities of the Plan target kind.

Application validation must establish at publication time:

- Tenant ownership;
- target kind;
- identity existence;
- duplicate elimination under canonical identity;
- lifecycle and publication eligibility rules;
- trigger prerequisites;
- any Product Authority-defined exclusion or blocking condition.

An external identifier may be used for lookup, but published coverage stores canonical Waiotech target identity.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--typed-selector"></a>
## Typed selector

A selector is a versioned Product Authority-defined structure. It is not a generic field/operator/value language.

The selector schema explicitly defines which governed facts may participate, normalization, validation, and interpretation.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--functional-location-selector-dimensions"></a>
### Functional Location selector dimensions

Permitted dimensions may include only explicitly supported facts such as:

- exact Functional Location branch;
- lifecycle;
- Operational Criticality;
- governed external-mapping criteria.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--asset-selector-dimensions"></a>
### Asset selector dimensions

Permitted dimensions may include only explicitly supported facts such as:

- Asset Classification;
- physical Asset subtree where Product Authority permits it;
- lifecycle;
- current installation within a Functional Location branch where explicitly supported;
- governed external-mapping criteria.

Selectors must not expose arbitrary SQL columns, JSON paths, free-form field names, executable expressions, browser-defined predicates, Team names as Plant semantics, or internal ORM structure.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--keep-selector-behavior-reviewable-and-deterministic"></a>
## Keep selector behavior reviewable and deterministic

For one normalized selector, Tenant state, evaluation instant, catalogue/reference-data revision, and selector contract version, target resolution must be deterministic.

Ordering used for review and digest generation is canonical and stable.

Selector evaluation must be Tenant-scoped before any other predicate can broaden the candidate set.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--preview-is-non-authoritative"></a>
## Preview is non-authoritative

A preview is a review artifact, not published coverage.

It includes at least:

- target kind;
- normalized selector or explicit-selection summary;
- selector contract version where applicable;
- evaluation time;
- included canonical target identities;
- excluded or ineligible candidates and typed reasons;
- missing trigger prerequisites;
- target-to-Measurement-Point binding where applicable;
- additions and removals relative to effective published coverage where applicable;
- deterministic reviewed-content digest;
- bounded count and truncation/limit meaning where relevant.

The browser must not construct its own candidate target set from partial registry data.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--resolve-operational-coverage-only-during-protected-publication"></a>
## Resolve operational coverage only during protected publication

Publication re-evaluates the proposed selection inside the authoritative Application transaction using the same typed resolution contract.

For selector-based authoring, the publication result must match the reviewed digest. If it does not, publication fails with an explicit target-drift result and returns or makes available a new reviewable preview.

The Server must never publish a target set materially different from the one the Actor reviewed merely because Plant facts changed between preview and publication.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--prevent-partially-published-target-sets"></a>
## Prevent partially published target sets

Target resolution and revision publication are atomic.

If any required target, trigger binding, or publication prerequisite cannot be established, the Plan Revision is not partially published unless Product Authority explicitly defines a reviewed exclusion rule whose result was part of the reviewed digest.

Database rows for some valid candidates do not justify silently dropping invalid candidates.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--a-resolved-plan-target"></a>
## A resolved Plan target

A resolved target is immutable published evidence owned by one Plan Revision.

It preserves at least:

- Plan Revision;
- target kind;
- canonical target identity;
- resolution provenance as explicit selection or selector result;
- publication-time relationship to the reviewed target-set digest;
- Product Authority-required snapshot facts needed for interpretation;
- resolved trigger Measurement Point where the trigger kind requires one.

Resolved target identity is not recalculated during ordinary occurrence generation.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--resolve-measurement-point-trigger-bindings-per-target"></a>
## Resolve Measurement Point trigger bindings per target

For a Measurement Point-triggered Plan, every target must resolve exactly one trigger Measurement Point before publication.

The candidate Measurement Point must:

- belong to the same Tenant;
- be active for publication use;
- observe that resolved target directly as its supported subject;
- use the measured quantity required by the trigger;
- have a canonical unit compatible with the trigger contract;
- satisfy any additional Product Authority-defined trigger eligibility rule.

Automatic selection is allowed only when exactly one eligible candidate exists.

Zero eligible candidates and several eligible candidates are both publication blockers until the author explicitly resolves the ambiguity or changes Plan coverage/configuration.

The preview and deterministic digest include each target-to-Measurement-Point binding. Publication persists the binding with the Plan Revision.

A later Measurement Point creation, retirement, source remapping, instrument replacement, Asset replacement, or Plant-model change never substitutes another trigger point inside an already-published revision.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--preserve-published-coverage-immutably"></a>
## Preserve published coverage immutably

After publication, Functional Location hierarchy, Asset composition, Asset Classification, Asset installation, lifecycle, Operational Criticality, Measurement Point configuration, and external mappings may change without rewriting the Plan Revision's published target set.

Historical operation uses the resolved target identity and trigger binding preserved by the Plan Revision.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--target-drift-is-a-derived-comparison"></a>
## Target drift is a derived comparison

Target drift compares current selector resolution with one published resolved target set at an explicit evaluation time.

It may identify:

- newly matching targets;
- targets that no longer match;
- retired or decommissioned targets;
- changed installation context;
- classification changes;
- missing or ambiguous trigger prerequisites;
- external-mapping differences;
- other Product Authority-defined causes.

Drift is review information only. It never mutates published coverage or creates/removes Scheduled Work.

A coverage change requires a new draft and reviewed Plan publication.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--separate-historical-coverage-from-current-action-eligibility"></a>
## Separate historical coverage from current action eligibility

A target may remain historical published coverage while becoming currently ineligible for new occurrence recognition or generation.

Occurrence evaluation therefore checks current eligibility through the owning Plan/Scheduled Work contract without altering the historical target row.

An ineligible published target produces an explicit planning, reconciliation, or disposition result where required. It must not silently disappear.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--do-not-rewrite-accepted-work-through-definition-publication"></a>
## Do not rewrite accepted work through definition publication

Publishing or superseding a Plan Revision never changes existing Scheduled Work or Work Orders.

Those records retain their original Plan Revision, Procedure Revision, resolved target, trigger identity, and evidence.

Where a business correction is required, it occurs through the affected record's explicit reconciliation or lifecycle action rather than by changing the source definition.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--make-omitted-coverage-explainable"></a>
## Make omitted coverage explainable

Every candidate excluded from reviewed coverage because of missing facts, invalid lifecycle, trigger incompatibility, authorization, or another product rule must have a typed explainable reason when the exclusion is material to publication review.

Unknown or unresolved meaning must not be translated into silent exclusion.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--bound-selector-cost-and-protect-tenant-isolation"></a>
## Bound selector cost and protect Tenant isolation

Selector evaluation uses indexed Plant facts, deterministic ordering, bounded result size, explicit timeout/resource limits, and Tenant predicates enforced below Application code where persistence authority requires it.

A selector that exceeds supported cost or size fails with a typed reviewable result rather than degrading into an unbounded query.

Selector evaluation must not expose records or aggregate counts from another Tenant through results, errors, timing-sensitive diagnostics, or generated previews.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--version-selector-structure-without-making-historical-operation-depend-on-it"></a>
## Version selector structure without making historical operation depend on it

Persisted selector definitions carry a contract version when interpretation can evolve.

A supported release may read earlier selector versions for review or migration according to Engineering Authority, but historical occurrence operation does not depend on re-running obsolete selector logic because published coverage is frozen as canonical resolved targets.

Selector normalization and digest calculation are deterministic and versioned together where required.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--end-live-selector-authority-at-publication"></a>
## End live selector authority at publication

After publication, the selector remains provenance and authoring history. It is not operational authority for automatic target addition/removal.

Ordinary Scheduled Work recognition reads the immutable resolved target set and applicable trigger bindings.

Re-evaluation is limited to preview, drift analysis, preparation of new unpublished changes, and explicit governance functions.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--verify-authoring-publication-and-operation-separately"></a>
## Verify authoring, publication, and operation separately

Tests must distinguish:

- selector/explicit-selection authoring and normalization;
- preview and digest generation;
- concurrency between preview and protected publication;
- all-or-nothing target and trigger-binding persistence;
- drift detection;
- historical reproducibility after Plant changes;
- occurrence generation from immutable coverage;
- Tenant isolation and bounded selector cost.

A passing selector query test does not prove publication or operational correctness.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-030-plan-target-resolution-md--related-documents"></a>
## Related documents

- [Procedures and Maintenance Plans](#doc-10-product-40-maintenance-040-procedures-and-maintenance-plans-md)
- [Scheduled Work](#doc-10-product-40-maintenance-050-scheduled-work-md)
- [Revisions and publication](#doc-20-engineering-20-data-020-revisions-and-publication-md)
- [Measurement Points](#doc-10-product-30-plant-050-measurement-points-md)

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md"></a>

<!-- Source: 20-engineering/20-data/040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction.md -->

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--consistency-concurrency-and-correction"></a>
# Consistency, concurrency, and correction

Authoritative writes use explicit transaction, lock, isolation, concurrency, projection, deletion, and additive-correction rules. Redis and replicas cannot become authoritative write paths.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--require-explicit-stale-write-protection-for-mutable-authoritative-state"></a>
## Require explicit stale-write protection for mutable authoritative state

Waiotech must require explicit stale-write protection for mutable authoritative state.

Mutable aggregates carry a monotonic concurrency version. Commands update through a compare-and-swap condition and reject stale versions without overwriting intervening changes.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--use-database-locking-only-where-it-directly-protects-an-invariant"></a>
## Use database locking only where it directly protects an invariant

Waiotech must use database locking only where it directly protects an invariant.

Row locks are used for contested records where the invariant depends on serialized access, including stock posting, unique process claims, publication contention, and bounded assignment transitions. Locks remain narrow and transactions remain short.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--do-not-use-advisory-locks-as-an-unbounded-global-mutex"></a>
## Do not use advisory locks as an unbounded global mutex

Waiotech must not use advisory locks as an unbounded global mutex.

Advisory locks are permitted for a defined coordination key when row ownership is unavailable or cross-row serialization is required. The key, acquisition order, timeout, and recovery behavior must be documented and tested.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--choose-isolation-from-invariant-requirements-and-test-real-concurrency"></a>
## Choose isolation from invariant requirements and test real concurrency

Choose isolation from invariant requirements and test real concurrency.

The command contract selects the lowest PostgreSQL isolation level that preserves its invariants. Serialization failures and deadlocks are classified and retried only when the command remains idempotent and safe.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--keep-command-truth-on-authoritative-postgresql"></a>
## Keep command truth on authoritative PostgreSQL

Waiotech must keep command truth on authoritative PostgreSQL.

Authoritative commands and Readiness evaluations use the primary authoritative database. Replica-backed queries are limited to contracts that declare tolerated lag and cannot affect protected action acceptance.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--keep-projections-rebuildable-and-subordinate"></a>
## Keep projections rebuildable and subordinate

Waiotech must keep projections rebuildable and subordinate.

A projection declares its authoritative sources, derivation, freshness, rebuild behavior, and explicit non-authority. Loss or staleness does not alter source records. Commands do not depend on a projection unless its consistency contract proves authoritative equivalence.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--do-not-hide-product-lifecycle-behind-row-deletion"></a>
## Do not hide product lifecycle behind row deletion

Waiotech must not hide product lifecycle behind row deletion.

Product lifecycle actions use cancellation, retirement, revocation, withdrawal, or deactivation as defined by Product Authority. Physical deletion is limited to disposable technical data or a separate approved destruction contract.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--make-data-correction-additive-attributable-and-verifiable"></a>
## Make data correction additive, attributable, and verifiable

Waiotech must make data correction additive, attributable, and verifiable.

A correction uses a typed protected command or migration that preserves original evidence, correction reason, Actor, effective and recorded time, affected relationships, audit, and resulting canonical state. Direct operational SQL is not an ordinary correction path.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--correct-process-evidence-without-erasing-provenance"></a>
## Correct Process evidence without erasing provenance

Process Reading, Operational Observation, Operational Action, and immutable Reliability evidence use additive correction or supersession according to Product Authority.

A corrected Reading preserves the original reported value, source, effective time, quality, and Actor. Derived current views and Maintenance trigger evaluation exclude invalidated evidence according to the canonical correction state without deleting historical source evidence.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--coordinate-cross-domain-consistency-in-application-transactions"></a>
## Coordinate cross-domain consistency in Application transactions

When one User intent affects several owning domains, the Application layer coordinates explicit domain commands inside the smallest safe transaction boundary or through a documented durable follow-up obligation.

No domain aggregate may mutate another domain's tables directly. For example, Process escalation may establish a Maintenance Work Request and relationship while Process retains Condition authority; Maintenance material use may establish Inventory custody movement while Maintenance retains usage meaning.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--use-database-constraints-as-part-of-the-product-preserving-implementation"></a>
## Use database constraints as part of the product-preserving implementation

Waiotech must use database constraints as part of the product-preserving implementation.

Migrations and continuous integration verify foreign keys, Tenant constraints, uniqueness, code domains, nullability, immutable relationships, revision effectivity, assignment integrity, Command Receipt uniqueness, outbox integrity, and artifact-to-Attachment relationships.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-040-consistency-concurrency-and-correction-md--related-documents"></a>
## Related documents
- [Persistence and Tenant isolation](#doc-20-engineering-20-data-010-persistence-and-tenant-isolation-md)
- [Commands and idempotency](#doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md)
- [Audit, events, and transactional outbox](#doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md)

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md"></a>

<!-- Source: 20-engineering/20-data/050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data.md -->

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--catalogues-and-generated-reference-data"></a>
# Catalogues and generated reference data

Product-owned catalogues use stable governed codes, canonical source definitions, localization, retirement, generation, API exposure, and migration rules. Tenant master data remains separate and cannot create executable product behavior.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--a-catalogue"></a>
## A catalogue

Waiotech must treat catalogue values as product contracts rather than editable labels.

A catalogue is a governed set of stable coded values used to classify, constrain, authorize, or interpret product behavior. It defines code, meaning, owner, applicability, lifecycle, relationships, and presentation metadata.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--a-taxonomy"></a>
## A taxonomy

Waiotech must use taxonomy only when structure is part of the contract.

A taxonomy is a governed classification structure whose values have hierarchical or semantic relationships. Not every catalogue is hierarchical, and not every hierarchy is product-fixed.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--do-not-infer-catalogue-ownership-from-use-in-a-dropdown-or-table"></a>
## Do not infer catalogue ownership from use in a dropdown or table

Waiotech must not infer catalogue ownership from use in a dropdown or table.

Tenant master data represents Tenant-owned operational identities such as Item, Item Category, Stock Location, Team, or another Product Authority concept. Product-owned catalogue values have one Waiotech-defined meaning across Tenants.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--declare-governance-explicitly-in-product-authority"></a>
## Declare governance explicitly in Product Authority

Declare governance explicitly in Product Authority.

Each governed value set is classified as:

1. product-fixed closed catalogue;
2. product-managed governed extension point;
3. Tenant-governed catalogue;
4. Tenant master data;
5. derived registry or reference.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--derive-governance-from-product-authority-only"></a>
## Derive governance from Product Authority only

Waiotech must derive governance from Product Authority only.

A database table, active flag, CRUD component, or localization support does not grant Tenant authority. Source-code storage does not independently prove product ownership.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--keep-tenant-purpose-closed-stable-and-product-owned"></a>
## Keep Tenant purpose closed, stable, and product-owned

Waiotech must keep Tenant purpose closed, stable, and product-owned.

Tenant purpose is a product-fixed closed catalogue owned by the Tenant domain with exactly `operational` and `learning`. Tenants cannot add, rename, reinterpret, or retire purpose codes. Persistence, APIs, generated clients, reporting exclusions, side-effect fences, and tests derive from the same canonical definition.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--use-one-product-wide-maintenance-discipline-catalogue"></a>
## Use one product-wide Maintenance discipline catalogue

Waiotech must use one product-wide Maintenance discipline catalogue.

One Waiotech catalogue defines every Maintenance discipline code and meaning. A Tenant may declare applicability to canonical codes but cannot create, rename, reinterpret, or retire disciplines.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--separate-tenant-applicability-from-catalogue-ownership"></a>
## Separate Tenant applicability from catalogue ownership

Waiotech must separate Tenant applicability from catalogue ownership.

A separate Tenant-to-canonical-code relationship controls ordinary selection. It does not create a Tenant-specific discipline or change historical records when applicability ends.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--keep-catalogue-meaning-module-owned"></a>
## Keep catalogue meaning module-owned

Waiotech must keep catalogue meaning module-owned.

The product module owning the meaning owns the canonical definition. A shared generator validates and combines definitions but does not become business owner.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--share-generation-infrastructure-without-creating-an-unowned-taxonomy-domain"></a>
## Share generation infrastructure without creating an unowned taxonomy domain

Share generation infrastructure without creating an unowned taxonomy domain.

Shared tooling may load, validate, localize, and generate catalogue representations. It cannot centralize unrelated product meanings into a universal catalogue service.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--the-canonical-source-format"></a>
## The canonical source format

Waiotech must maintain one reviewable source of truth for every product-owned catalogue.

A catalogue uses one source-controlled typed definition owned by its module. The source declares catalogue identity, code, canonical meaning, lifecycle, localization key, relationships, dependencies, and generation requirements. The wastewater taxonomy is owned by `server/reference-data/taxonomy/catalogue.json` and validated and generated by `server/scripts/generate_taxonomy.py`.

Generated representations include Server validation metadata, read-only PostgreSQL reference rows seeded by the canonical migration, Dashboard TypeScript, Android Kotlin where the Android contract consumes the canonical definition, localization coverage, and contract tests. Generated database rows have no runtime CRUD authority and must match the governed source digest.

Operational-code metadata is explicit. A taxonomy encoding token does not automatically become an entity-code token. Categories declare whether and how a value contributes to a module-owned operational-code policy. The Server allocates entity codes transactionally; browsers only display a non-reserving pattern.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--use-language-independent-stable-machine-identities"></a>
## Use language-independent stable machine identities

Waiotech must use language-independent stable machine identities.

Ordinary catalogue codes use stable lowercase ASCII `snake_case` within their owning catalogue. Permission and action codes use their module-qualified dotted standards. Codes do not contain labels, Tenant prefixes, or database sequence meaning.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--protect-historical-records-events-reports-exports-and-integrations-from-code-drift"></a>
## Protect historical records, events, reports, exports, and integrations from code drift

Waiotech must protect historical records, events, reports, exports, and integrations from code drift.

A used code is permanent identity. A label may change when meaning remains unchanged. A new meaning requires a new code. A retired code remains reserved and interpretable.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--avoid-a-global-catalogue-version-that-obscures-code-level-compatibility"></a>
## Avoid a global catalogue version that obscures code-level compatibility

Avoid a global catalogue version that obscures code-level compatibility.

Stable codes and preserved definitions provide historical interpretation. Generated manifests may carry source commit, digest, generator identity, and schema version as provenance.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--separate-identity-and-meaning-from-presentation-language"></a>
## Separate identity and meaning from presentation language

Waiotech must separate identity and meaning from presentation language.

Localization uses stable keys derived from catalogue and code. APIs and persistence use canonical code. Missing translations use the governed fallback locale and fail verification when coverage is mandatory.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--treat-addition-as-a-governed-product-contract-change"></a>
## Treat addition as a governed product-contract change

Waiotech must treat addition as a governed product-contract change.

Addition requires Product Authority support, module-owned source change, stable code, behavior implementation where applicable, localization, generated output, persistence support, contract-version review, migrations, and tests.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--apply-retirement-prospectively-and-preserve-history"></a>
## Apply retirement prospectively and preserve history

Waiotech must apply retirement prospectively and preserve history.

Retirement prevents unsupported new use while preserving historical reads, audit, events, reports, exports, and integration interpretation. Retirement never deletes or reassigns the code.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--do-not-silently-reinterpret-historical-codes"></a>
## Do not silently reinterpret historical codes

Waiotech must not silently reinterpret historical codes.

Only when Product Authority declares semantic equivalence for the affected records. Otherwise, conversion requires an explicit correction or migration preserving original and replacement evidence.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--surface-unknown-codes-as-integrity-defects"></a>
## Surface unknown codes as integrity defects

Surface unknown codes as integrity defects.

Unknown input is rejected. Unknown persisted product-owned code is a data-integrity non-conformance and is not coerced to `other`, empty, or a similar value.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--prohibit-generic-escape-values-without-a-domain-contract"></a>
## Prohibit generic escape values without a domain contract

Waiotech must prohibit generic escape values without a domain contract.

`Other` exists only when Product Authority defines its meaning, supporting text, reporting effect, and behavioral limits.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--keep-reasons-tied-to-the-action-whose-meaning-they-explain"></a>
## Keep reasons tied to the action whose meaning they explain

Waiotech must keep reasons tied to the action whose meaning they explain.

Each action family owns its reason-code catalogue. A generic global reason table is prohibited. Each family declares ownership, extension authority, supporting text, lifecycle, reporting meaning, and integration behavior.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--keep-permission-meaning-module-owned-and-assembly-centralized"></a>
## Keep Permission meaning module-owned and assembly centralized

Waiotech must keep Permission meaning module-owned and assembly centralized.

Product modules define protected Permissions. IAM assembles a generated catalogue with stable code, owner, description, dependencies, protected actions, sensitivity, and lifecycle. Tenants cannot create Permission codes.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--separate-control-definition-from-tenant-configured-value"></a>
## Separate control definition from Tenant-configured value

Waiotech must separate control definition from Tenant-configured value.

Tenant Control types are product-owned typed definitions declaring code, value type, constraints, dependencies, publication validation, impact behavior, and sensitivity. Tenants configure values through Tenant Control Policy but cannot create control types.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--generate-every-repeated-representation-from-canonical-definitions"></a>
## Generate every repeated representation from canonical definitions

Waiotech must generate every repeated representation from canonical definitions.

Generation may produce backend types, validation metadata, database reference synchronization, OpenAPI values, TypeScript and Kotlin values, localization manifests, read-only catalogue responses, documentation, version manifests, and test fixtures.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--generate-only-representations-required-by-the-contract"></a>
## Generate only representations required by the contract

Waiotech must generate only representations required by the contract.

Output depends on use. A small closed value may generate types. A rich Permission catalogue may generate metadata and queries. Tenant-owned master data uses ordinary persistence and APIs.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--use-database-tables-as-derived-persistence-representations"></a>
## Use database tables as derived persistence representations

Waiotech may use database tables as derived persistence representations when relational constraints or rich metadata require them. Rows remain synchronized from canonical source and have no Tenant CRUD authority.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--prohibit-direct-editing-of-product-owned-reference-rows"></a>
## Prohibit direct editing of product-owned reference rows

Waiotech must prohibit direct editing of product-owned reference rows.

Changes occur through canonical source, approved migration or synchronization, verified generation, and release.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--keep-api-identity-language-independent-and-governance-safe"></a>
## Keep API identity language-independent and governance-safe

Waiotech must keep API identity language-independent and governance-safe.

Commands submit canonical codes. Read responses may add localization key, label, description, owner, group, and active or retired condition. Product-fixed catalogue endpoints are read-only.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--model-tenant-governed-values-as-real-domain-concepts"></a>
## Model Tenant-governed values as real domain concepts

Waiotech must model Tenant-governed values as real domain concepts.

Each is an explicit module-owned entity and API contract defining Tenant identity, code scope, lifecycle, hierarchy, Permission, history, import, export, and retirement. A universal `custom_value` table is prohibited.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--prevent-ungoverned-behavior-from-attaching-to-configurable-values"></a>
## Prevent ungoverned behavior from attaching to configurable values

Waiotech must prevent ungoverned behavior from attaching to configurable values.

Only when Product Authority explicitly defines that behavior. A label or category cannot grant Permission, change lifecycle, alter Plan resolution, post Inventory, or invoke arbitrary automation.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--make-catalogue-drift-release-blocking"></a>
## Make catalogue drift release-blocking

Waiotech must make catalogue drift release-blocking.

Continuous integration verifies source validity, unique codes, no code removal or reassignment, dependency resolution, localization, generated parity, OpenAPI and client parity, database reference parity, and absence of forbidden Tenant CRUD.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--use-evidence-backed-mappings-and-normalize-aliases-before-canonical-storage"></a>
## Use evidence-backed mappings and normalize aliases before canonical storage

Waiotech must use evidence-backed mappings and normalize aliases before canonical storage.

Each value is classified as exact match, approved alias, distinct canonical code, Tenant master data, or unsupported ambiguity. Migration preserves original value, mapping, reason, provenance, affected records, and unresolved cases.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-20-data-050-catalogues-and-generated-reference-data-md--related-documents"></a>

Commands, idempotency, audit, events, APIs, OpenAPI, and generated clients.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md"></a>

<!-- Source: 20-engineering/30-actions-and-contracts/010-commands-and-idempotency.md -->

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--commands-and-idempotency"></a>
# Commands and idempotency

Every accepted mutation is a named command with explicit context, action code, authorization, guards, transaction boundary, idempotency, operation identity, and recoverable receipt semantics.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--a-command"></a>
## A command

Waiotech must represent every product mutation through an explicit Application command.

A command is a named request to perform one product-significant mutation. It identifies the owned action, typed input, Actor context, Tenant or platform scope, authority, lifecycle guards, Readiness, transaction, result, audit, events, and retry behavior.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--use-stable-action-identities-across-api-audit-events-and-generated-references"></a>
## Use stable action identities across API, audit, events, and generated references

Waiotech must use stable action identities across API, audit, events, and generated references.

Action codes use stable module-qualified identities such as `maintenance.work_order.release` or `inventory.movement.issue`. The code is independent from route names, labels, handler names, and implementation classes.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--establish-authoritative-execution-context-before-command-evaluation"></a>
## Establish authoritative execution context before command evaluation

Establish authoritative execution context before command evaluation.

The command context contains:

- authenticated principal;
- effective Tenant or platform scope;
- Actor type and identity;
- request identity;
- correlation identity;
- idempotency key where required;
- expected concurrency version where required;
- recorded time from the trusted clock;
- support or system-authority context where applicable.

Clients cannot assert Permission, effective Access Profile, lifecycle outcome, audit identity, or event identity.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--the-canonical-command-execution-sequence"></a>
## The canonical command execution sequence

Waiotech must keep command acceptance atomic and ordered.

The sequence is:

```text
authenticate
-> establish Tenant or platform context
-> acquire or inspect Command Receipt
-> authorize
-> load authoritative state
-> verify concurrency
-> evaluate lifecycle and Readiness
-> execute Domain behavior
-> persist all mandatory consequences
-> record Domain Events and required audit
-> create outbox obligations only for declared durable consumers
-> finalize Command Receipt
-> commit
-> return the accepted result
```

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--make-retry-safety-explicit-for-every-mutation-boundary"></a>
## Make retry safety explicit for every mutation boundary

Waiotech must make retry safety explicit for every mutation boundary.

Every externally retryable mutation with business consequences requires idempotency. This includes browser actions, integration commands, artifact creation, publication, posting, assignment changes, support actions, and process creation. A purely internal transaction may omit a client key when duplicate invocation is impossible by contract.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--the-scope-of-an-idempotency-key"></a>
## The scope of an idempotency key

Waiotech must prevent key collision across authority and action boundaries.

The key is scoped by effective Tenant or platform scope, authenticated principal class, action code, and any additional stable boundary required by the command contract. It is opaque, bounded, contains no secret, and remains stable for one logical submission.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--a-request-fingerprint"></a>
## A request fingerprint

Waiotech must use fingerprints to distinguish replay from conflicting key reuse.

The fingerprint is a deterministic digest of the command's effective normalized input, including subject identities and business inputs but excluding transport noise such as request identity. Secret values are normalized without being stored in clear form.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--a-command-receipt"></a>
## A Command Receipt

Waiotech must use durable receipts to establish one result for one logical attempt.

A Command Receipt is the durable record of one idempotent logical command attempt. It preserves:

- receipt identity;
- scope;
- principal;
- action code;
- idempotency key digest;
- request fingerprint;
- processing claim;
- established result or rejection;
- operation identity;
- created and completed times;
- reconciliation condition where required.

The receipt does not become domain evidence unless Product Authority assigns that meaning.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--keep-receipt-state-technical-and-separate-from-product-lifecycle"></a>
## Keep receipt state technical and separate from product lifecycle

Waiotech must keep receipt state technical and separate from product lifecycle.

A receipt may represent bounded technical conditions such as:

```text
processing
succeeded
rejected
failed_retryable
failed_internal
reconciliation_required
```

The state describes command execution, not the lifecycle of the subject.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--prevent-abandoned-receipts-from-blocking-safe-replay-indefinitely"></a>
## Prevent abandoned receipts from blocking safe replay indefinitely

Waiotech must prevent abandoned receipts from blocking safe replay indefinitely.

A processing claim has a bounded lease. When execution terminates before commit, the claim expires and the same logical command may resume. The claim does not permit a second concurrent executor while valid.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--replay-one-authoritative-result-rather-than-re-executing-the-command"></a>
## Replay one authoritative result rather than re-executing the command

Replay one authoritative result rather than re-executing the command.

Waiotech returns the established result without repeating accepted business consequences. The response may indicate idempotent replay.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--protect-key-identity-from-semantic-reuse"></a>
## Protect key identity from semantic reuse

Waiotech must protect key identity from semantic reuse.

The command is rejected with `platform.idempotency.conflict`. The established receipt and original result remain unchanged. The original payload is not exposed.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--make-negative-outcomes-duplicate-safe-without-fabricating-accepted-actions"></a>
## Make negative outcomes duplicate-safe without fabricating accepted actions

Waiotech must make negative outcomes duplicate-safe without fabricating accepted actions.

A deterministic business rejection may be stored in the Command Receipt so a retry returns the same rejection while the receipt contract applies. A rejection creates no partial domain consequence. Material security denials may create separate audit evidence.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--distinguish-rollback-response-loss-and-cross-system-uncertainty"></a>
## Distinguish rollback, response loss, and cross-system uncertainty

Waiotech must distinguish rollback, response loss, and cross-system uncertainty.

When the transaction did not commit because of an explicitly classified transient infrastructure failure, the API returns `service.command_failed_retryable` with category `retryable_infrastructure`, `retryable: true`, and the established operation identity. PostgreSQL classification is limited to known connection, serialization, deadlock, lock-availability, and shutdown conditions; constraint failures and arbitrary exceptions are not transient. A deliberate retry reuses the same idempotency key and may reclaim the receipt. An unexpected programming, invariant, or mapping failure establishes `failed_internal`, returns a sanitized non-retryable `500`, and is not automatically re-executed by receipt replay. A connection failure raised while committing an otherwise successful command has an unknown outcome: the API returns `platform.command.reconciliation_required` with the operation identity and must not classify the command as a safe pre-commit retry. Reusing the same idempotency key then reconciles against any committed receipt without repeating accepted business consequences. When the transaction committed but the response was lost, replay returns the established result. When an external outcome is uncertain, the receipt or related process enters reconciliation-required handling.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--operation-identity"></a>
## Operation identity

Waiotech must use operation identity as the execution spine of a command.

Operation identity identifies one logical command execution across requests, receipt replay, audit, events, outbox delivery, Worker processing, and external reconciliation. The backend establishes it and preserves it across retries of the same logical attempt.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--keep-execution-identities-distinct-and-propagate-only-those-applicable-to-each-boundary"></a>
## Keep execution identities distinct and propagate only those applicable to each boundary

Waiotech must keep execution identities distinct and propagate only those applicable to each boundary.

Request identity identifies one transport attempt. Correlation identity groups a broader flow. Operation identity identifies one logical command. Causation identity identifies the immediate command or event that produced another fact. Trace identity remains technical observability context.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-010-commands-and-idempotency-md--related-documents"></a>

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md"></a>

<!-- Source: 20-engineering/30-actions-and-contracts/020-audit-events-and-outbox.md -->

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--audit-events-and-transactional-outbox"></a>
# Audit, events, and transactional outbox

Required audit facts and durable domain events commit with the authoritative state change. The transactional outbox provides recoverable at-least-once delivery without turning delivery order into hidden business authority.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--an-audit-record"></a>
## An audit record

Waiotech must record who exercised protected authority without copying unrestricted payloads.

An audit record is durable accountability evidence for a protected action or security change. It preserves the Actor, authority path, Tenant or platform scope, action code, subject, reason, accepted or denied outcome where required, recorded time, operation identity, and safe changed-fact summary.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--do-not-accept-an-auditable-action-without-durable-accountability"></a>
## Do not accept an auditable action without durable accountability

Waiotech must not accept an action requiring audit without durable accountability. Required audit is written in the same PostgreSQL transaction as the accepted action or denial it records.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--keep-audit-append-only-and-independently-interpretable"></a>
## Keep audit append-only and independently interpretable

Waiotech must keep audit append-only and independently interpretable.

Corrections are additive and reference the original record. Physical destruction requires a separate explicit legal and Product Authority contract.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--a-domain-event"></a>
## A domain event

Emit facts rather than imperative integration commands from Domain ownership.

Persisting a Domain Event does not by itself create an asynchronous delivery obligation. An outbox destination is declared only when a declared internal consumer owns a required durable consequence. Waiotech does not create a default catch-all `internal` destination, and the outbox is not a second audit log.

A domain event is an immutable accepted fact owned by the module whose Domain action established it. It names what happened, not what a consumer should do.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--use-stable-fact-oriented-event-names"></a>
## Use stable fact-oriented event names

Waiotech must use stable fact-oriented event names.

Event types use `<module>.<subject>.<past_tense_fact>`, for example `maintenance.work_order.released`. A new business fact receives a new event type.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--version-durable-payload-schemas-without-embedding-versions-in-event-type-names"></a>
## Version durable payload schemas without embedding versions in event type names

Version durable payload schemas without embedding versions in event type names.

Persisted, outbox, replayable, cross-module, or external messages may be processed after a deployment, retry, dead-letter recovery, or consumer upgrade. The envelope therefore carries `schema_version` so consumers can interpret the payload structure safely.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--separate-payload-compatibility-from-fact-identity"></a>
## Separate payload compatibility from fact identity

Waiotech must separate payload compatibility from fact identity.

Compatible optional additions may retain the version when consumers are required to ignore unknown fields. Removing, renaming, changing requiredness, changing type, changing structure, or changing meaning requires another schema version. A different business fact requires another event type.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--use-one-stable-event-envelope-for-durable-messaging"></a>
## Use one stable event envelope for durable messaging

Waiotech must use one stable event envelope for durable messaging.

The envelope contains, where applicable:

- event identity;
- event type;
- schema version;
- source module;
- Tenant or platform scope;
- subject type and identity;
- occurred time;
- recorded time;
- Actor context;
- operation identity;
- Command Receipt identity;
- correlation identity;
- causation identity;
- typed payload.

It excludes secrets and unrestricted entity snapshots.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--the-transactional-outbox"></a>
## The transactional outbox

Guarantee that an accepted fact cannot lose its required asynchronous consequence.

The outbox is a PostgreSQL record created in the same transaction as the source fact. It preserves the event and destination-specific delivery obligations. External publication occurs only after commit.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--design-every-event-consumer-for-duplicate-delivery"></a>
## Design every event consumer for duplicate delivery

Waiotech must design every event consumer for duplicate delivery.

Delivery is at least once. Consumers must be duplicate-safe through an inbox, processed-event record, unique constraint, or equivalent durable mechanism. Exactly-once transport claims do not replace application idempotency.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--preserve-only-necessary-bounded-ordering"></a>
## Preserve only necessary bounded ordering

Waiotech must preserve only necessary bounded ordering.

Ordering is declared only where product or integration meaning requires it. The contract identifies the ordering key, ordinarily one subject or stream, and consumers reject or reconcile impossible sequence. Global ordering is prohibited.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--avoid-event-payloads-becoming-uncontrolled-replica-models"></a>
## Avoid event payloads becoming uncontrolled replica models

Avoid event payloads becoming uncontrolled replica models. Events ordinarily contain the accepted fact and references needed by consumers rather than complete aggregate snapshots. A specific contract may require an immutable snapshot when replay or independent interpretation requires it. Consumers query owning modules through approved contracts when more data is required.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--apply-versioning-where-compatibility-risk-exists"></a>
## Apply versioning where compatibility risk exists

Waiotech must apply explicit schema versioning to any message that is persisted, replayable, or exchanged across an independently deployable boundary. Purely in-process ephemeral messages may omit schema version when they cannot survive or cross that boundary.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-020-audit-events-and-outbox-md--related-documents"></a>
## Related documents

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md"></a>

<!-- Source: 20-engineering/30-actions-and-contracts/030-api-contracts.md -->

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--api-contracts"></a>
# API contracts

Waiotech exposes resource-oriented reads and named action commands through explicit, Tenant-scoped, typed HTTP contracts. Representations, errors, available actions, concurrency, idempotency, and diagnostics remain stable and precise.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--use-resources-for-identity-and-named-endpoints-for-product-significant-mutations"></a>
## Use resources for identity and named endpoints for product-significant mutations

Waiotech must use resources for identity and named endpoints for product-significant mutations.

Waiotech uses resource-oriented HTTP APIs with named business-action endpoints for protected transitions. Resources express stable concepts; actions express governed commands.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--align-api-ownership-with-product-module-ownership"></a>
## Align API ownership with product-module ownership

Align API ownership with product-module ownership.

The module owning the product concept owns routes, schemas, commands, queries, errors, action metadata, versioning, and OpenAPI representation. Shared API code owns only technical transport standards.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--keep-http-transport-subordinate-to-use-case-contracts"></a>
## Keep HTTP transport subordinate to use-case contracts

Waiotech must keep HTTP transport subordinate to use-case contracts.

It parses and maps transport. Application and Domain own authority, lifecycle, Readiness, evidence, transactions, and events.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--version-incompatible-external-commitments-not-deployments"></a>
## Version incompatible external commitments, not deployments

Version incompatible external commitments, not deployments.

One canonical major API base is used. Before any retained external deployment, an incorrect contract is corrected directly. After a retained external deployment, an incompatible commitment requires an explicit new major version with its own complete contract and migration plan; aliases, dual shapes, and hidden translation inside the canonical version are prohibited. Application releases do not create decorative API versions.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--use-canonical-product-authority-vocabulary-in-routes"></a>
## Use canonical Product Authority vocabulary in routes

Waiotech must use canonical Product Authority vocabulary in routes.

Paths use lowercase plural canonical nouns with hyphens, such as `/functional-locations`, `/assets`, `/process-units`, `/measurement-points`, `/process-routines`, `/process-rounds`, `/process-conditions`, `/work-orders`, `/failure-events`, `/replenishment-requests`, and `/access-profiles`. Obsolete or ambiguous terminology is prohibited.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--make-generated-clients-and-diagnostics-unambiguous"></a>
## Make generated clients and diagnostics unambiguous

Waiotech must make generated clients and diagnostics unambiguous.

OpenAPI path parameters use explicit names such as `{work_order_id}` rather than generic `{id}`.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--use-shallow-stable-resource-addressing"></a>
## Use shallow stable resource addressing

Waiotech must use shallow stable resource addressing.

Nesting is bounded to relationships whose meaning depends on the parent. Stable independently addressable entities receive their own canonical routes. Paths do not reproduce the database graph.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--map-http-methods-to-product-semantics-rather-than-generic-crud"></a>
## Map HTTP methods to product semantics rather than generic CRUD

Map HTTP methods to product semantics rather than generic CRUD.

GET performs safe reads. POST creates resources or invokes named commands. PATCH is limited to typed bounded edits of explicitly mutable non-lifecycle fields. PUT is used only for true complete replacement. DELETE is used only for disposable technical data or Product Authority physical deletion.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--prohibit-storage-shaped-mutation-apis"></a>
## Prohibit storage-shaped mutation APIs

Waiotech must prohibit storage-shaped mutation APIs.

Each edit uses a typed request contract. Lifecycle, derived state, authority, and immutable evidence are never assignable through patch documents.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--never-accept-client-assertions-of-authority-or-derived-state"></a>
## Never accept client assertions of authority or derived state

Waiotech must never accept client assertions of authority or derived state.

Clients submit legitimate business input, selected canonical identities, reasons, notes, expected version, idempotency key, and supported effective-time facts. The backend derives Actor, Tenant validity, Permission, lifecycle result, Readiness, audit, and event metadata.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--require-one-validated-tenant-or-platform-context-per-operation"></a>
## Require one validated Tenant or platform context per operation

Waiotech must require one validated Tenant or platform context per operation.

One consistent explicit mechanism, such as a dedicated header or route boundary, identifies the requested Tenant. The backend validates it against the authenticated principal. Platform operations use explicit platform routes and scope.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--keep-machine-process-reading-ingress-explicit"></a>
## Keep machine Process Reading ingress explicit

Machine Reading submission uses an explicit Process-owned API contract authenticated as an Integration Principal.

The transport may support bounded single and batch submission, but each accepted Reading is validated against canonical Measurement Point identity or a governed Measurement Source Mapping before Application invocation. The API must not accept arbitrary tag names as canonical plant identity.

Machine ingress preserves source event identity where available, effective time, reported value and unit, quality, Data Source, Integration Principal, and idempotency semantics required to make retries duplicate-safe.

Human Dashboard Reading entry uses ordinary human Tenant authority and may use a different transport route or request shape. Both paths converge on the same Process Application and Domain Reading semantics before acceptance.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--enforce-one-tenant-boundary-per-ordinary-operation"></a>
## Enforce one Tenant boundary per ordinary operation

Waiotech must enforce one Tenant boundary per ordinary operation.

Every identity, Attachment, reference, result, and consequence belongs to the effective Tenant. Cross-Tenant platform operations use dedicated contracts.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--use-one-stable-language-independent-wire-naming-convention"></a>
## Use one stable language-independent wire naming convention

Waiotech must use one stable language-independent wire naming convention.

Wire properties use lowercase `snake_case`. Generated clients may map to language conventions internally without changing the wire contract.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--keep-technical-identity-opaque"></a>
## Keep technical identity opaque

Waiotech must keep technical identity opaque.

Technical identifiers are opaque UUID strings. Operational codes are separate properties. Clients do not construct or infer meaning from UUIDs.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--exchange-unambiguous-time-semantics"></a>
## Exchange unambiguous time semantics

Exchange unambiguous time semantics.

Recorded instants use RFC 3339 with explicit offset and ordinarily normalize to UTC in responses. Local dates, local times, and timezone identifiers remain distinct fields.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--preserve-exact-values-across-clients"></a>
## Preserve exact values across clients

Waiotech must preserve exact values across clients.

Exact decimal values are serialized as strings when a JSON number could lose precision. The schema declares precision, scale, unit, and currency meaning.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--make-partial-input-semantics-explicit"></a>
## Make partial input semantics explicit

Waiotech must make partial input semantics explicit.

Omission means the contract does not provide or change a value. `null` means explicit absence where the schema permits it. Edit contracts define this distinction per field.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--reject-unrecognized-command-input-by-default"></a>
## Reject unrecognized command input by default

Waiotech must reject unrecognized command input by default unless a typed extension object is explicitly defined. Unknown fields indicate drift, typo, or unsupported meaning.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--permit-additive-enrichment-without-silent-meaning-change"></a>
## Permit additive enrichment without silent meaning change

Permit additive enrichment without silent meaning change.

Response fields may be added only when consumers are required to ignore unknown fields and the addition does not change interpretation. Semantically controlling fields require an explicit contract-version decision before release.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--expose-product-contracts-rather-than-persistence-models"></a>
## Expose product contracts rather than persistence models

Waiotech must expose product contracts rather than persistence models.

Representations contain product-significant identity, lifecycle, relationships, effective definition references, concurrency version, Readiness, blockers, available actions, and evidence references as applicable. They exclude secrets, ORM shape, private delivery state, and arbitrary metadata bags.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--use-purpose-specific-read-models-rather-than-one-oversized-entity-schema"></a>
## Use purpose-specific read models rather than one oversized entity schema

Waiotech must use purpose-specific read models rather than one oversized entity schema.

Summary, detail, history, command result, export, and administrative views may differ through explicit schemas.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--return-the-accepted-product-result-rather-than-only-transport-acknowledgement"></a>
## Return the accepted product result rather than only transport acknowledgement

Return the accepted product result rather than only transport acknowledgement.

A command response contains the affected subject, operation and receipt identities, replay indicator, warnings, and authoritative concurrency version when the command establishes one. A purpose-specific response may add a typed bounded result such as a credential or upload instruction. Ordinary clients refetch the authoritative read model after mutation rather than receiving an arbitrary command payload bag.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--do-not-use-202-for-non-durable-receipt-of-a-request"></a>
## Do not use `202` for non-durable receipt of a request

Waiotech must not use `202` for non-durable receipt of a request.

Only when the command transaction durably establishes a long-running process. The response identifies the process resource, state, operation, retrieval link, and cancellation action where supported.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--use-status-and-typed-errors-consistently"></a>
## Use status and typed errors consistently

Waiotech must use status and typed errors consistently.

HTTP status communicates broad transport category. Stable Waiotech error codes communicate exact meaning. Common mappings include 400 malformed input, 401 invalid authentication, 403 denied authority, 404 safe non-disclosure, 409 lifecycle/concurrency/idempotency conflict, 422 typed business-input validation, 429 rate limit, and 503 safe temporary unavailability.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--the-error-envelope"></a>
## The error envelope

Waiotech must keep errors machine-readable and non-disclosing.

The error envelope contains stable code, category, safe message, correlation identity, operation identity where available, field violations, typed blockers, named safe references, and retryability. Safe detail fields are closed and explicit; arbitrary metadata is prohibited. It never exposes stack traces, SQL, secrets, or infrastructure topology.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--keep-client-behavior-independent-from-message-wording"></a>
## Keep client behavior independent from message wording

Waiotech must keep client behavior independent from message wording.

Domain errors use `<module>.<subject>.<condition>`. Technical cross-cutting errors use a stable platform namespace.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--expose-one-stable-validation-contract"></a>
## Expose one stable validation contract

Waiotech must expose one stable validation contract.

Each violation identifies a typed input path and stable code. Framework-specific error structures are mapped into the Waiotech envelope. Transport locations such as `body`, `query`, `path`, `header`, and `cookie` are not part of the input-model path exposed to clients.

Each field violation exposes a stable `code`, an input-model `path`, a localization `message_key`, and an optional closed `parameters` object containing only schema-declared safe scalar display values. Clients localize from `message_key` and `parameters`; they must not branch on English message text. The Server may also provide a safe fallback message for unsupported clients. Arbitrary validation metadata and raw rejected payloads are prohibited.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--an-available-action"></a>
## An available action

Waiotech must use authoritative action metadata for client usability.

An available action is a backend-evaluated statement that one named action is available to the Actor for the subject at evaluation time. It may include label key, method, route, input schema, idempotency requirement, and concurrency requirement.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--revalidate-every-action-at-execution-time"></a>
## Revalidate every action at execution time

Revalidate every action at execution time.

The command re-evaluates authority, state, concurrency, Readiness, policy, and references because facts may change.

<a id="doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-030-api-contracts-md--related-documents"></a>
## Related documents
- [API queries, files, batches, and revisions](#doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-040-api-queries-files-and-batches-md)
- [OpenAPI and generated clients](#doc-20-engineering-30-actions-and-contracts-050-openapi-and-generated-clients-md)
